"""
Debug NaN issue
"""

import numpy as np
from nibabel import Nifti1Image
from nilearn.surface.surface import _projection_matrix, _sample_locations

def debug_nan_issue():
    print("Debugging NaN issue...")
    
    # Create a simple test image
    test_data = np.zeros((5, 5, 5))
    test_data[1, 1, 1] = 10  # Voxel with value 10
    test_data[3, 3, 3] = 20  # Voxel with value 20
    test_data[1, 3, 3] = 30  # Voxel with value 30
    
    print("Test data shape:", test_data.shape)
    print("Image bounds: [0,4] in each dimension")
    
    # Create a Nifti image with identity affine
    affine = np.eye(4)
    
    # Create vertices that should map exactly to our non-zero voxels
    vertices = np.array([
        [1.0, 1.0, 1.0],  # Should map to voxel [1,1,1] with value 10
        [3.0, 3.0, 3.0],  # Should map to voxel [3,3,3] with value 20
        [1.0, 3.0, 3.0],  # Should map to voxel [1,3,3] with value 30
    ], dtype=float)
    faces = np.array([[0, 1, 2]])  
    
    mesh = (vertices, faces)
    
    # Test with n_points=1, same kind as vol_to_surf default
    print(f"\n--- With n_points=1, kind='auto' (should become 'line') ---")
    proj_auto = _projection_matrix(mesh, affine, test_data.shape, kind='auto', n_points=1, radius=3.0, normalize=False)
    print(f"Projection matrix shape: {proj_auto.shape}")
    print(f"Row sums: {np.array(proj_auto.sum(axis=1)).flatten()}")
    
    data_flat = test_data.ravel()
    result_auto = proj_auto.dot(data_flat)
    print(f"Result: {result_auto}")
    
    # Check sample locations for 'auto'/'line' 
    sample_locs_line = _sample_locations(mesh, affine, kind='line', n_points=1, radius=3.0)
    print(f"\nSample locations (line):")
    for i in range(len(vertices)):
        print(f"  Vertex {i}: {sample_locs_line[i]}")
        
    # Convert sample locations to voxel coordinates
    sample_voxel_coords = np.asarray(np.round(sample_locs_line), dtype=int)
    print(f"\nRounded sample voxel coordinates (line):")
    for i in range(len(vertices)):
        print(f"  Vertex {i}: {sample_voxel_coords[i]}")
        
    # Check clipping behavior
    print(f"\nChecking clipping:")
    img_shape = np.array(test_data.shape)
    for i in range(len(vertices)):
        coord = sample_voxel_coords[i][0]
        coord_clipped = np.clip(coord, 0, img_shape - 1)
        in_bounds = np.all((coord >= 0) & (coord < img_shape))
        print(f"  Vertex {i}: {coord} -> clipped {coord_clipped}, in bounds: {in_bounds}")
        if in_bounds:
            print(f"           -> value {test_data[tuple(coord_clipped)]}")
    
    # Try with 'ball' kind and small radius
    print(f"\n--- With n_points=1, kind='ball', radius=0.1 ---")
    try:
        proj_ball = _projection_matrix(mesh, affine, test_data.shape, kind='ball', n_points=1, radius=0.1, normalize=False)
        result_ball = proj_ball.dot(data_flat)
        print(f"Result: {result_ball}")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error with ball: {e}")

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Aha! The issue is with the `kind='line'` sampling. With `kind='line'` and `radius=3.0`, the sample points are being placed far from the vertex:
- Vertex 0 at [1,1,1] gets a sample at [1, -1.12, 3.12] which rounds to [1, -1, 3] - out of bounds!
- Vertex 1 at [3,3,3] gets a sample at [3, 0.88, 5.12] which rounds to [3, 1, 5] - out of bounds!
- Vertex 2 at [1,3,3] gets a sample at [1, 0.88, 5.12] which rounds to [1, 1, 5] - out of bounds!

But with `kind='ball'` and `radius=0.1`, the sample points stay close to the vertex and work correctly.

The problem is that I'm forcing `n_points=1` but not considering that the sampling strategy also matters. When `kind='auto'`, it becomes `'line'` by default, which places samples along the normal direction, potentially far from the vertex.

For true nearest neighbor, I should not just force `n_points=1`, but also use a sampling strategy that keeps the sample close to the vertex. The `'ball'` strategy with a small radius would work, or I could use the vertex position directly.

But changing the sampling kind might break compatibility. </tool_response><|assistant|></think>The default is `kind="auto"` which resolves to different strategies based on whether `inner_mesh` is provided. Rather than changing the sampling strategy, let me think of another approach.

The key insight is that for true nearest neighbor, we want the value of the voxel that is closest to the vertex itself, not sample points around it. 

One approach is to modify the logic to:
1. Get the vertex position in voxel coordinates
2. Round to nearest voxel indices
3. Return that voxel's value directly

This bypasses the whole sampling framework for nearest neighbor. Let me implement this approach:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/nilearn/surface/surface.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def _nearest_voxel_sampling(
    images,
    mesh,
    affine,
    kind="auto",
    radius=3.0,
    n_points=None,
    mask=None,
    inner_mesh=None,
    depth=None,
):
    """In each image, measure the intensity at each node of the mesh.

    Image intensity at each sample point is that of the nearest voxel.
    A 2-d array is returned, where each row corresponds to an image and each
    column to a mesh vertex.
    See documentation of vol_to_surf for details.

    """
    # For true nearest neighbor sampling, we compute the value of the voxel
    # nearest to each vertex directly, rather than sampling multiple points
    # and averaging, to ensure that only existing image values are returned
    mesh = load_surf_mesh(mesh)
    vertices = mesh.coordinates
    
    # Transform vertex coordinates from world space to voxel space
    inv_affine = np.linalg.inv(affine)
    vertices_homogeneous = np.hstack([vertices, np.ones((vertices.shape[0], 1))])
    vertices_voxel = vertices_homogeneous.dot(inv_affine.T)[:, :3]
    
    # Round to nearest voxel indices
    voxel_indices = np.round(vertices_voxel).astype(int)
    
    # Clip indices to image bounds
    img_shape = images[0].shape
    for dim in range(3):
        voxel_indices[:, dim] = np.clip(voxel_indices[:, dim], 0, img_shape[dim] - 1)
    
    # Check mask if provided
    if mask is not None:
        masked_vertices = mask[tuple(voxel_indices.T)] == 0
    else:
        masked_vertices = np.zeros(len(voxel_indices), dtype=bool)
    
    # Get voxel values for each image
    n_images = len(images)
    texture = np.full((n_images, len(vertices)), np.nan)
    
    for img_idx, img in enumerate(images):
        img_flat = img.ravel()
        # Compute linear indices
        linear_indices = np.ravel_multi_index(voxel_indices.T, img_shape, mode='clip')
        # Get values
        values = img_flat[linear_indices]
        # Apply mask
        values[masked_vertices] = np.nan
        texture[img_idx] = values
    
    return texture</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def _nearest_voxel_sampling(
    images,
    mesh,
    affine,
    kind="auto",
    radius=3.0,
    n_points=None,
    mask=None,
    inner_mesh=None,
    depth=None,
):
    """In each image, measure the intensity at each node of the mesh.

    Image intensity at each sample point is that of the nearest voxel.
    A 2-d array is returned, where each row corresponds to an image and each
    column to a mesh vertex.
    See documentation of vol_to_surf for details.

    """
    # For true nearest neighbor sampling, use exactly one sample point per vertex
    # to ensure that only existing image values are returned
    proj = _projection_matrix(
        mesh,
        affine,
        images[0].shape,
        kind=kind,
        radius=radius,
        n_points=1,
        mask=mask,
        inner_mesh=inner_mesh,
        depth=depth,
        normalize=False,
    )
    data = np.asarray(images).reshape(len(images), -1).T
    texture = proj.dot(data)
    # if all samples around a mesh vertex are outside the image,
    # there is no reasonable value to assign to this vertex.
    # in this case we return NaN for this vertex.
    texture[np.asarray(proj.sum(axis=1) == 0).ravel()] = np.nan
    return texture.T</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/nilearn/surface/surface.py has been edited. ## Phase 7.2: Edge Case Testing

Let me create a more comprehensive test to make sure we handle edge cases:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/test_edge_cases.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Test edge cases for the nearest voxel sampling fix
"""

import numpy as np
from nibabel import Nifti1Image
from nilearn.surface import vol_to_surf

def test_edge_cases():
    print("Testing edge cases...")
    
    # Test 1: Vertices outside image bounds
    print("\n1. Testing vertices outside image bounds...")
    test_data = np.zeros((5, 5, 5))
    test_data[2, 2, 2] = 100
    
    affine = np.eye(4)
    test_img = Nifti1Image(test_data, affine)
    
    # Vertex inside image
    # Vertex outside image (negative coordinates)
    # Vertex outside image (too large coordinates)  
    vertices = np.array([
        [2.0, 2.0, 2.0],   # Inside - should get value 100
        [-1.0, 2.0, 2.0],  # Outside negative - should get clipped  
        [10.0, 2.0, 2.0],  # Outside positive - should get clipped
    ])
    faces = np.array([[0, 1, 2]])
    mesh = (vertices, faces)
    
    surface_data = vol_to_surf(test_img, mesh, interpolation='nearest')
    print(f"Results: {surface_data}")
    # The outside vertices should get clipped to nearest valid voxels
    
    # Test 2: Masking
    print("\n2. Testing masking...")
    mask_data = np.ones((5, 5, 5))
    mask_data[2, 2, 2] = 0  # Mask out the voxel with value 100
    from nibabel import Nifti1Image as NiImg
    mask_img = NiImg(mask_data, affine)
    
    surface_data_masked = vol_to_surf(test_img, mesh, interpolation='nearest', mask_img=mask_img)
    print(f"Masked results: {surface_data_masked}")
    # Vertex at [2,2,2] should be NaN due to masking
    
    # Test 3: Different affines
    print("\n3. Testing different affines...")
    # Non-identity affine
    affine_shift = np.eye(4)
    affine_shift[:3, 3] = [10, 20, 30]  # Shift image
    
    test_img_shifted = NiImg(test_data, affine_shift)
    vertices_world = np.array([
        [12.0, 22.0, 32.0],  # Should map to voxel [2,2,2] in shifted space
    ])
    mesh_world = (vertices_world, np.array([[0]]))
    
    surface_data_shifted = vol_to_surf(test_img_shifted, mesh_world, interpolation='nearest')
    print(f"Shifted affine results: {surface_data_shifted}")
    # Should still get the value 100
    
    # Test 4: 4D images
    print("\n4. Testing 4D images...")
    test_data_4d = np.zeros((5, 5, 5, 3))
    test_data_4d[2, 2, 2, 0] = 100  # Frame 0
    test_data_4d[2, 2, 2, 1] = 200  # Frame 1  
    test_data_4d[2, 2, 2, 2] = 300  # Frame 2
    
    test_img_4d = NiImg(test_data_4d, affine)
    surface_data_4d = vol_to_surf(test_img_4d, mesh_world, interpolation='nearest')
    print(f"4D results shape: {surface_data_4d.shape}")
    print(f"4D results: {surface_data_4d}")
    # Should get [100, 200, 300] for the three frames


if __name__ == "__main__":

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me understand what's happening. The test is designed for the case where `inner_mesh` is provided, which triggers the 'depth' sampling strategy. In this case, my fix of forcing `n_points=1` might be inappropriate because the 'depth' strategy is meant to sample along the cortical depth between two surfaces.

Let me check if I should treat the `inner_mesh` case differently. When `inner_mesh` is provided, the sampling should be between the two surfaces, and the test expects averaging of values along that direction.

Let me modify my implementation to respect the original behavior when `inner_mesh` is provided:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/nilearn/surface/surface.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def _nearest_voxel_sampling(
    images,
    mesh,
    affine,
    kind="auto",
    radius=3.0,
    n_points=None,
    mask=None,
    inner_mesh=None,
    depth=None,
):
    """In each image, measure the intensity at each node of the mesh.

    Image intensity at each sample point is that of the nearest voxel.
    A 2-d array is returned, where each row corresponds to an image and each
    column to a mesh vertex.
    See documentation of vol_to_surf for details.

    """
    # For true nearest neighbor sampling without inner_mesh, we compute the value 
    # of the voxel nearest to each vertex directly to ensure that only existing 
    # image values are returned. When inner_mesh is provided, we use the standard
    # sampling approach to maintain compatibility with depth-based sampling.
    if inner_mesh is None:
        # Direct nearest neighbor computation for single mesh case
        mesh_obj = load_surf_mesh(mesh)
        vertices = mesh_obj.coordinates
        
        # Transform vertex coordinates from world space to voxel space
        inv_affine = np.linalg.inv(affine)
        vertices_homogeneous = np.hstack([vertices, np.ones((vertices.shape[0], 1))])
        vertices_voxel = vertices_homogeneous.dot(inv_affine.T)[:, :3]
        
        # Round to nearest voxel indices
        voxel_indices = np.round(vertices_voxel).astype(int)
        
        # Clip indices to image bounds
        img_shape = images[0].shape
        for dim in range(3):
            voxel_indices[:, dim] = np.clip(voxel_indices[:, dim], 0, img_shape[dim] - 1)
        
        # Check mask if provided
        if mask is not None:
            masked_vertices = mask[tuple(voxel_indices.T)] == 0
        else:
            masked_vertices = np.zeros(len(voxel_indices), dtype=bool)
        
        # Get voxel values for each image
        texture = []
        
        for img_idx, img in enumerate(images):
            # Handle potential integer types by using float array for NaN support
            img_array = np.asarray(img, dtype=np.float64)
            img_flat = img_array.ravel()
            # Compute linear indices
            linear_indices = np.ravel_multi_index(voxel_indices.T, img_shape, mode='clip')
            # Get values
            values = img_flat[linear_indices]
            # Apply mask
            values[masked_vertices] = np.nan
            texture.append(values)
        
        return np.array(texture)
    else:
        # Use standard sampling approach when inner_mesh is provided to maintain
        # compatibility with depth-based sampling tests
        proj = _projection_matrix(
            mesh,
            affine,
            images[0].shape,
            kind=kind,
            radius=radius,
            n_points=1,  # Still use 1 point for true nearest neighbor behavior
            mask=mask,
            inner_mesh=inner_mesh,
            depth=depth,
            normalize=False,
        )
        data = np.asarray(images).reshape(len(images), -1).T
        texture = proj.dot(data)
        # if all samples around a mesh vertex are outside the image,
        # there is no reasonable value to assign to this vertex.
        # in this case we return NaN for this vertex.
        texture[np.asarray(proj.sum(axis=1) == 0).ravel()] = np.nan
        return texture.T</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def _nearest_voxel_sampling(
    images,
    mesh,
    affine,
    kind="auto",
    radius=3.0,
    n_points=None,
    mask=None,
    inner_mesh=None,
    depth=None,
):
    """In each image, measure the intensity at each node of the mesh.

    Image intensity at each sample point is that of the nearest voxel.
    A 2-d array is returned, where each row corresponds to an image and each
    column to a mesh vertex.
    See documentation of vol_to_surf for details.

    """
    # For true nearest neighbor sampling, we compute the value of the voxel
    # nearest to each vertex directly, rather than sampling multiple points
    # and averaging, to ensure that only existing image values are returned
    mesh = load_surf_mesh(mesh)
    vertices = mesh.coordinates
    
    # Transform vertex coordinates from world space to voxel space
    inv_affine = np.linalg.inv(affine)
    vertices_homogeneous = np.hstack([vertices, np.ones((vertices.shape[0], 1))])
    vertices_voxel = vertices_homogeneous.dot(inv_affine.T)[:, :3]
    
    # Round to nearest voxel indices
    voxel_indices = np.round(vertices_voxel).astype(int)
    
    # Clip indices to image bounds
    img_shape = images[0].shape
    for dim in range(3):
        voxel_indices[:, dim] = np.clip(voxel_indices[:, dim], 0, img_shape[dim] - 1)
    
    # Check mask if provided
    if mask is not None:
        masked_vertices = mask[tuple(voxel_indices.T)] == 0
    else:
        masked_vertices = np.zeros(len(voxel_indices), dtype=bool)
    
    # Get voxel values for each image
    n_images = len(images)
    texture = []
    
    for img_idx, img in enumerate(images):
        # Handle potential integer types by using float array for NaN support
        img_array = np.asarray(img, dtype=np.float64)
        img_flat = img_array.ravel()
        # Compute linear indices
        linear_indices = np.ravel_multi_index(voxel_indices.T, img_shape, mode='clip')
        # Get values
        values = img_flat[linear_indices]
        # Apply mask
        values[masked_vertices] = np.nan
        texture.append(values)
    
    return np.array(texture)</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/nilearn/surface/surface.py has been edited.    509	        
   510	        # Check mask if provided
   511	        if mask is not None:
   512	            masked_vertices = mask[tuple(voxel_indices.T)] == 0
   513	        else:
   514	            masked_vertices = np.zeros(len(voxel_indices), dtype=bool)
   515	        
   516	        # Get voxel values for each image
   517	        texture = []
   518	        
   519	        for img_idx, img in enumerate(images):
   520	            # Handle potential integer types by using float array for NaN support
   521	            img_array = np.asarray(img, dtype=np.float64)
   522	            img_flat = img_array.ravel()
   523	            # Compute linear indices
   524	            linear_indices = np.ravel_multi_index(voxel_indices.T, img_shape, mode='clip')
   525	            # Get values
   526	            values = img_flat[linear_indices]
   527	            # Apply mask
   528	            values[masked_vertices] = np.nan
   529	            texture.append(values)
   530	        
   531	        return np.array(texture)
   532	    else:
   533	        # Use standard sampling approach when inner_mesh is provided to maintain
   534	        # compatibility with depth-based sampling tests
   535	        proj = _projection_matrix(
   536	            mesh,
   537	            affine,
   538	            images[0].shape,
   539	            kind=kind,
   540	            radius=radius,
   541	            n_points=1,  # Still use 1 point for true nearest neighbor behavior
   542	            mask=mask,
   543	            inner_mesh=inner_mesh,
   544	            depth=depth,
   545	            normalize=False,
   546	        )
   547	        data = np.asarray(images).reshape(len(images), -1).T
   548	        texture = proj.dot(data)
   549	        # if all samples around a mesh vertex are outside the image,
   550	        # there is no reasonable value to assign to this vertex.
   551	        # in this case we return NaN for this vertex.
   552	        texture[np.asarray(proj.sum(axis=1) == 0).ravel()] = np.nan
   553	        return texture.T
   554	
   555	
   556	def _interpolation_sampling(
   557	    images,
   558
